===Original===

**This book**

I dedicate, with all my heart, to every man or

woman who may derive from it, in however

small a degree, the impulse of

unselfish public service

===Chinese===

谨将此书全心全意献给每一位不论程度深浅、能从中汲取无私投身公共事业动力的人

## Preface / 序言
===Original===

I have not written a book since the campaign. I did not write this book at all. It is the result of the editorial literary skill of Mr. William Bayard Hale, who has put together here in their right sequences the more suggestive portions of my campaign speeches.
And yet it is not a book of campaign speeches. It is a discussion of a number of very vital subjects in the free form of extemporaneously spoken words. I have left the sentences in the form in which they were stenographically reported. I have not tried to alter the easygoing and often colloquial phraseology in which they were uttered from the platform, in the hope that they would seem the more fresh and spontaneous because of their very lack of pruning and recasting. They have been suffered to run their unpremeditated course even at the cost of such repetition and redundancy as the extemporaneous speaker apparently inevitably falls into.
The book is not a discussion of measures or of programs. It is an attempt to express the new spirit of our politics and to set forth, in large terms which may stick in the imagination, what it is that must be done if we are to restore our politics to their full spiritual vigor again, and our national life, whether in trade, in industry, or in what concerns us only as families and individuals, to its purity, its self-respect, and its pristine strength and freedom. The New Freedom is only the old revived and clothed in the unconquerable strength of modern America.
Woodrow Wilson

===Chinese===

竞选结束以来，我并未撰写过任何书籍。确切地说，这本书根本算不上由我写就。它是威廉·贝亚德·黑尔（William Bayard Hale）先生编辑与文学才华的结晶。他将我竞选演说中最具启发性的部分，按恰当的脉络汇集成编。

然而，这并非一本普通的竞选演说集。它是以即兴口语的自由形式，对诸多极其重大的议题展开的探讨。我保留了这些句子在速记记录中的本来面貌。我并未试图修改当初在演讲台（the platform）上口头表述时那种随性且往往口语化的措辞，旨在使它们正因未加修剪与重塑，而显得更加清新自然。即便即兴演说者难免会落入重复与赘述的窠臼，我也听任它们保持未作预设的本色。

本书并非探讨具体的举措或方案，而是力图表达我们政治生活中的新精神，并以能引起深刻遐想的宏阔视角，阐明若要使我们的政治重获充沛的精神活力，并使我们的国家生活——无论是在贸易、工业，还是仅关涉家庭与个人的层面——重归纯洁、自尊，以及原初的力量与自由，我们究竟必须做些什么。「新自由」（The New Freedom）不过是在现代美国不可战胜的力量中重获新生、重披铠甲的昔日自由。

伍德罗·威尔逊（Woodrow Wilson）

## I The Old Order Changeth / 旧秩序正在改变
===Original===

There is one great basic fact which underlies all the questions that are discussed on the political platform at the present moment. That singular fact is that nothing is done in this country as it was done twenty years ago.
We are in the presence of a new organization of society. Our life has broken away from the past. The life of America is not the life that it was twenty years ago; it is not the life that it was ten years ago. We have changed our economic conditions, absolutely, from top to bottom; and, with our economic society, the organization of our life. The old political formulas do not fit the present problems; they read now like documents taken out of a forgotten age. The older cries sound as if they belonged to a past age which men have almost forgotten. Things which used to be put into the party platforms of ten years ago would sound antiquated if put into a platform now. We are facing the necessity of fitting a new social organization, as we did once fit the old organization, to the happiness and prosperity of the great body of citizens; for we are conscious that the new order of society has not been made to fit and provide the convenience or prosperity of the average man. The life of the nation has grown infinitely varied. It does not centre now upon questions of governmental structure or of the distribution of governmental powers. It centres upon questions of the very structure and operation of society itself, of which government is only the instrument. Our development has run so fast and so far along the lines sketched in the earlier day of constitutional definition, has so crossed and interlaced those lines, has piled upon them such novel structures of trust and combination, has elaborated within them a life so manifold, so full of forces which transcend the boundaries of the country itself and fill the eyes of the world, that a new nation seems to have been created which the old formulas do not fit or afford a vital interpretation of.
We have come upon a very different age from any that preceded us. We have come upon an age when we do not do business in the way in which we used to do business﻿—when we do not carry on any of the operations of manufacture, sale, transportation, or communication as men used to carry them on. There is a sense in which in our day the individual has been submerged.

===Chinese===

当前在政治舞台（political platform）上讨论的所有问题背后，都存在着一个重大的基本事实。这一非同寻常的事实便是：在这个国家，没有任何事情的运转方式还与20年前（twenty years ago）相同。
我们正面对着一种全新的社会组织形式。我们的生活已同过去决裂。今日美国的生活不再是20年前的生活，也不是10年前（ten years ago）的生活。我们从上至下彻底改变了自身的经济条件；而伴随着经济社会的转变，我们生活的组织方式也发生了剧变。旧有的政治信条已不再契合当下的问题；它们如今读来犹如来自被遗忘时代的陈年旧档。曾经的呼声听上去仿佛属于一个人们几乎已淡忘的逝去纪元。10年前写入政党竞选纲领（platform）的内容，倘若放到今天的纲领中，必将显得陈腐不堪。我们正面临这样一种必然性：正如我们昔日曾使旧有组织契合广大公民的幸福与繁荣（prosperity）那样，如今我们必须使全新的社会组织契合广大公民的福祉；因为我们清醒地意识到，新的社会秩序并非为了普通人的便利或繁荣而建立。整个国家的生机变得无限多样化。当下的焦点不再是政府体制或政府权力的分配问题，而是社会本身的结构与运作问题——政府不过是其中的工具而已。我们在宪法界定初期所勾勒的轨迹上，发展得如此迅猛且深远，以至于极大地跨越并交织了那些界限，在其上层叠堆砌起前所未有的托拉斯与辛迪加联合结构，并在其中孕育出如此纷繁复杂、充满超越国界并令世界瞩目的磅礴力量的生活，以至于一个崭新的国家似乎由此诞生，而旧有的准则已无法契合它，亦无法对其作出富有活力的阐释。
我们步入了一个与以往任何时代都截然不同的纪元。在这个时代，我们开展商业活动的方式已不同以往——我们开展制造、销售、运输或通信的任何运作，都不再遵循人们以往的运作方式。从某种意义上说，在我们这个时代，个人已被淹没。

===Original===

In most parts of our country men work, not for themselves, not as partners in the old way in which they used to work, but generally as employees﻿—in a higher or lower grade﻿—of great corporations. There was a time when corporations played a very minor part in our business affairs, but now they play the chief part, and most men are the servants of corporations.
You know what happens when you are the servant of a corporation. You have in no instance access to the men who are really determining the policy of the corporation. If the corporation is doing the things that it ought not to do, you really have no voice in the matter and must obey the orders, and you have oftentimes with deep mortification to cooperate in the doing of things which you know are against the public interest. Your individuality is swallowed up in the individuality and purpose of a great organization.
It is true that, while most men are thus submerged in the corporation, a few, a very few, are exalted to a power which as individuals they could never have wielded. Through the great organizations of which they are the heads, a few are enabled to play a part unprecedented by anything in history in the control of the business operations of the country and in the determination of the happiness of great numbers of people.
Yesterday, and ever since history began, men were related to one another as individuals. To be sure there were the family, the Church, and the State, institutions which associated men in certain wide circles of relationship. But in the ordinary concerns of life, in the ordinary work, in the daily round, men dealt freely and directly with one another. Today, the everyday relationships of men are largely with great impersonal concerns, with organizations, not with other individual men.
Now this is nothing short of a new social age, a new era of human relationships, a new stage-setting for the drama of life.
---
In this new age we find, for instance, that our laws with regard to the relations of employer and employee are in many respects wholly antiquated and impossible. They were framed for another age, which nobody now living remembers, which is, indeed, so remote from our life that it would be difficult for many of us to understand it if it were described to us.

===Chinese===

在我们国家的大多数地区，人们工作不再是为自己干，也不再像过去那样以合伙人的旧方式共事，而通常是作为大公司中职位或高或低的雇员。曾几何时，公司在我们的商业事务中只扮演着微不足道的角色，但如今它们占据了主导地位，大多数人皆沦为公司的仆从。
你很清楚当你是公司的仆从时会发生什么。你绝不可能接触到真正决定公司政策的人物。倘若公司在做不该做的事，你实际上毫无发言权，只能服从命令，而且你往往不得不怀着深深的屈辱感，去协助做那些你深知有违公共利益的事情。你的个性被吞噬在庞大组织的个性与宗旨之中。
诚然，正当大多数人就这样被淹没在公司之中时，极少数人却被擢升到了作为个人原本绝无可能掌握的权力巅峰。借助他们所掌管的庞大组织，这极少数人在掌控国家商业运作、决定广大人民（the people）福祉方面，发挥着历史上空前绝后的作用。
昨天，以及自历史开端以来的漫长岁月里，人们都是作为个体相互发生联系的。固然有家庭、教会和国家等制度将人们联系在特定的广泛关系圈中；但在日常生活的寻常事务中，在普通的工作与日常往来中，人们彼此自由且直接地打交道。今天，人们日常交往的对象大多是冷冰冰的庞大机构与组织，而非其他鲜活的个人。
这不折不扣是一个新的社会时代，一个崭新的人类关系纪元，一出人生戏剧的全新舞台布景。
---
在这个新时代，我们发现，例如我们关于劳资关系的法律在诸多方面已完全过时且难以为继。那些法律是为另一个时代制定的，如今健在的人没有谁还记得那个时代；事实上，那个时代距离我们的生活是如此遥远，如果有人向我们描述它，我们当中的许多人都难以理解。

===Original===

The employer is now generally a corporation or a huge company of some kind; the employee is one of hundreds or of thousands brought together, not by individual masters whom they know and with whom they have personal relations, but by agents of one sort or another. Workingmen are marshaled in great numbers for the performance of a multitude of particular tasks under a common discipline. They generally use dangerous and powerful machinery, over whose repair and renewal they have no control. New rules must be devised with regard to their obligations and their rights, their obligations to their employers and their responsibilities to one another. Rules must be devised for their protection, for their compensation when injured, for their support when disabled.
There is something very new and very big and very complex about these new relations of capital and labor. A new economic society has sprung up, and we must effect a new set of adjustments. We must not pit power against weakness. The employer is generally, in our day, as I have said, not an individual, but a powerful group; and yet the workingman when dealing with his employer is still, under our existing law, an individual.
Why is it that we have a labor question at all? It is for the simple and very sufficient reason that the laboring man and the employer are not intimate associates now as they used to be in time past. Most of our laws were formed in the age when employer and employees knew each other, knew each other’s characters, were associates with each other, dealt with each other as man with man. That is no longer the case. You not only do not come into personal contact with the men who have the supreme command in those corporations, but it would be out of the question for you to do it. Our modern corporations employ thousands, and in some instances hundreds of thousands, of men. The only persons whom you see or deal with are local superintendents or local representatives of a vast organization, which is not like anything that the workingmen of the time in which our laws were framed knew anything about. A little group of workingmen, seeing their employer every day, dealing with him in a personal way, is one thing, and the modern body of labor engaged as employees of the huge enterprises that spread all over the country, dealing with men of whom they can form no personal conception, is another thing.

===Chinese===

如今的雇主通常是公司或某种巨型企业；雇员则是成百上千乃至成千上万被聚集在一起的人，将他们召集在一起的并非他们熟识并保持私人关系的个别雇主，而是各色各样的代理人。工人们在大规模组织下接受统一纪律约束，以完成繁多而具体的任务。他们通常操作危险而强力的机械，对于这些机械的维修与更新，他们毫无掌控权。对于他们的义务与权利、他们对雇主的责任以及他们彼此之间的责任，必须制定新的规则。必须制定用于保护他们的规则，规定他们在受伤时获得赔偿、在伤残时获得抚恤的规则。
在资本与劳动的新型关系中，存在着某种极其新颖、极其庞大且极其复杂的要素。一个全新的经济社会已经崛起，我们必须实施一系列新的调整。我们绝不能让强权与弱小互相对抗。正如我所言，在我们这个时代，雇主通常不再是个人，而是一个强大的集团；然而在现行法律下，工人在与雇主打交道时，依然被视为单个的个体。
为什么我们竟然会面临劳工问题？原因非常简单且极其充分：如今的劳动者与雇主之间，不再像过去那样是亲密的伙伴。我们的大多数法律都是在雇主与雇员彼此相识、熟知对方性格、结为伙伴、作为人与人直接打交道的时代形成的。如今情况已非如此。你不仅无法与那些在公司中掌握最高指挥权的人物建立私人接触，而且这种接触对你而言根本毫无可能。我们现代的公司雇用了数千人，在某些情况下甚至是数十万人。你所见到的或与之打交道的唯一对象，只是一个庞大组织的当地主管或地方代表，这与我们制定法律时代的工人们所知晓的任何事物都截然不同。一小群工人每天与雇主相见，以个人方式与他打交道，这是一回事；而遍布全国的巨型企业的广大劳工雇员，与那些在他们观念中无法具象化的人物打交道，则是完全另一回事。

===Original===

A very different thing. You never saw a corporation, any more than you ever saw a government. Many a workingman today never saw the body of men who are conducting the industry in which he is employed. And they never saw him. What they know about him is written in ledgers and books and letters, in the correspondence of the office, in the reports of the superintendents. He is a long way off from them.
So what we have to discuss is, not wrongs which individuals intentionally do﻿—I do not believe there are a great many of those﻿—but the wrongs of a system. I want to record my protest against any discussion of this matter which would seem to indicate that there are bodies of our fellow-citizens who are trying to grind us down and do us injustice. There are some men of that sort. I don’t know how they sleep o’ nights, but there are men of that kind. Thank God, they are not numerous. The truth is, we are all caught in a great economic system which is heartless. The modern corporation is not engaged in business as an individual. When we deal with it, we deal with an impersonal element, an immaterial piece of society. A modern corporation is a means of cooperation in the conduct of an enterprise which is so big that no one man can conduct it, and which the resources of no one man are sufficient to finance. A company is formed; that company puts out a prospectus; the promoters expect to raise a certain fund as capital stock. Well, how are they going to raise it? They are going to raise it from the public in general, some of whom will buy their stock. The moment that begins, there is formed﻿—what? A joint stock corporation. Men begin to pool their earnings, little piles, big piles. A certain number of men are elected by the stockholders to be directors, and these directors elect a president. This president is the head of the undertaking, and the directors are its managers.
Now, do the workingmen employed by that stock corporation deal with that president and those directors? Not at all. Does the public deal with that president and that board of directors? It does not. Can anybody bring them to account? It is next to impossible to do so. If you undertake it you will find it a game of hide and seek, with the objects of your search taking refuge now behind the tree of their individual personality, now behind that of their corporate irresponsibility.

===Chinese===

这是截然不同的两回事。你从未见过公司，正如你从未见过政府一样。今天许多工人从未见过管理雇用他们所在行业的那些人群，而那些人也从未见过工人的面孔。管理者对工人的了解，全写在分类账、簿册、信函、办公室公文以及主管的报告之中。工人与他们相隔甚远。
因此，我们要讨论的并非个人蓄意造成的过错——我相信这类过错并不多——而是制度的弊端。我谨在此抗议任何暗示有同行公民蓄意碾压我们并对我们施加不公的论调。确实有那一类人，我不知道他们夜里何以能安然入睡，但那种人确实存在。感谢上帝，他们终究只是少数。真相在于，我们所有人都被困在一个冷酷无情的庞大经济体制之中。现代公司并非作为个体从事商业活动。当我们与它打交道时，我们面对的是一种非人格化的要素，是社会中无形的一部分。现代公司是在经营一项极其庞大、以至于单凭一人之力无法操办、且无任何个人资力足以支持的企业时所采用的合作手段。一家公司成立了，它发布招股说明书，发起人期望筹集特定资金作为股本。那么他们如何筹集呢？他们将从广大公众中筹集，公众中的一些人会购买其股票。一旦这一进程启动，便形成了什么？一个股份公司。人们开始把自己的收入汇集在一起，汇聚成或小或大的资本堆。股东选出一定数量的人担任董事，董事们再选出一位总裁。这位总裁是企业的领头人，董事们则是其经营者。
那么，受雇于这家股份公司的工人会与那位总裁和董事们打交道吗？根本不会。公众会与总裁和董事会打交道吗？也不会。有人能向他们问责吗？这几乎是不可能的。如果你试图这么做，你会发现这就像一场捉迷藏的游戏：你寻找的目标一会儿躲在个人身份的树木后面，一会儿又藏匿在公司的法团免责盾牌之后。

===Original===

And do our laws take note of this curious state of things? Do they even attempt to distinguish between a man’s act as a corporation director and as an individual? They do not. Our laws still deal with us on the basis of the old system. The law is still living in the dead past which we have left behind. This is evident, for instance, with regard to the matter of employers’ liability for workingmen’s injuries. Suppose that a superintendent wants a workman to use a certain piece of machinery which it is not safe for him to use, and that the workman is injured by that piece of machinery. Some of our courts have held that the superintendent is a fellow-servant, or, as the law states it, a fellow-employee, and that, therefore, the man cannot recover damages for his injury. The superintendent who probably engaged the man is not his employer. Who is his employer? And whose negligence could conceivably come in there? The board of directors did not tell the employee to use that piece of machinery; and the president of the corporation did not tell him to use that piece of machinery. And so forth. Don’t you see by that theory that a man never can get redress for negligence on the part of the employer? When I hear judges reason upon the analogy of the relationships that used to exist between workmen and their employers a generation ago, I wonder if they have not opened their eyes to the modern world. You know, we have a right to expect that judges will have their eyes open, even though the law which they administer hasn’t awakened.
Yet that is but a single small detail illustrative of the difficulties we are in because we have not adjusted the law to the facts of the new order.
---
Since I entered politics, I have chiefly had men’s views confided to me privately. Some of the biggest men in the United States, in the field of commerce and manufacture, are afraid of somebody, are afraid of something. They know that there is a power somewhere so organized, so subtle, so watchful, so interlocked, so complete, so pervasive, that they had better not speak above their breath when they speak in condemnation of it.

===Chinese===

我们的法律注意到了这种奇特的事态吗？法律是否哪怕尝试过区分一个人作为公司董事的行为与作为个人的行为？根本没有。我们的法律仍然基于旧制度来对待我们。法律依然停留在我们已经抛诸身后的陈腐过去。例如，在雇主对工伤责任的问题上，这一点表现得尤为明显。假设一位主管要求工人操作某台对他而言并不安全的机器，而该工人因那台机器受了伤。我们的某些法院竟判定主管属于“共同雇从”（fellow-servant），或者正如法律所称的“共同雇员”，因此该工人无法为其所受伤害获得赔偿。当初雇用该工人的那位主管并非他的雇主。那么谁是他的雇主？在何种意义上又可能存在谁的过失？董事会并没有指示该雇员操作那台机器，公司总裁也没有指示他操作那台机器。依此类推。难道你们看不出，按照那种理论，一个人永远无法就雇方的疏忽过失获得救济吗？当我听到法官们援引上一代工人和雇主之间曾存在的关系来进行类推时，我禁不住怀疑他们是否未曾睁眼看过现代世界。你们知道，我们完全有理由期待法官能够睁开双眼，哪怕他们所适用的法律本身尚未觉醒。
然而，这不过是说明我们因未使法律适应新秩序现实而陷入困境的一个微小细节而已。
---
自我踏入政界以来，人们主要私下向我倾吐其见解。在商业和制造业领域，美国一些极具影响力的大人物却在害怕某个人，害怕某种势力。他们深知，在某个地方存在着一股组织如此严密、精微、警惕、盘根错节、完备且无孔不入的力量，以至于他们在出言谴责它时，最好连说话都不要高过耳语。

===Original===

They know that America is not a place of which it can be said, as it used to be, that a man may choose his own calling and pursue it just as far as his abilities enable him to pursue it; because today, if he enters certain fields, there are organizations which will use means against him that will prevent his building up a business which they do not want to have built up; organizations that will see to it that the ground is cut from under him and the markets shut against him. For if he begins to sell to certain retail dealers, to any retail dealers, the monopoly will refuse to sell to those dealers, and those dealers, afraid, will not buy the new man’s wares.
And this is the country which has lifted to the admiration of the world its ideals of absolutely free opportunity, where no man is supposed to be under any limitation except the limitations of his character and of his mind; where there is supposed to be no distinction of class, no distinction of blood, no distinction of social status, but where men win or lose on their merits.
I lay it very close to my own conscience as a public man whether we can any longer stand at our doors and welcome all newcomers upon those terms. American industry is not free, as once it was free; American enterprise is not free; the man with only a little capital is finding it harder to get into the field, more and more impossible to compete with the big fellow. Why? Because the laws of this country do not prevent the strong from crushing the weak. That is the reason, and because the strong have crushed the weak the strong dominate the industry and the economic life of this country. No man can deny that the lines of endeavor have more and more narrowed and stiffened; no man who knows anything about the development of industry in this country can have failed to observe that the larger kinds of credit are more and more difficult to obtain, unless you obtain them upon the terms of uniting your efforts with those who already control the industries of the country; and nobody can fail to observe that any man who tries to set himself up in competition with any process of manufacture which has been taken under the control of large combinations of capital will presently find himself either squeezed out or obliged to sell and allow himself to be absorbed.
There is a great deal that needs reconstruction in the United States.

===Chinese===

他们深知，美国已不再是一个可以像过去那样任由一个人自由选择职业、并在其能力所及范围内尽情施展的国度；因为在今天，如果他涉足某些领域，就会有组织对他采取手段，阻止他建立起他们不愿看到其建立的企业；那些组织会确保釜底抽薪，切断他的根基，封锁他的市场。因为一旦他开始向某些零售商销售，向任何零售商销售，垄断（monopoly）集团就会拒绝向这些零售商供货，而心怀恐惧的零售商便不敢采购这位新人的商品。
然而，正是这个国家，曾向全世界树立起让世人赞叹的绝对自由机遇的理想：在这里，任何人除了受到自身品格与才智的限制外，不应受到任何羁绊；在这里，不应存在阶级之分、血统之分、社会地位之分，每个人全凭自身才干赢取胜负。
作为一名公众人物，我扪心自问，我们是否还能站在国门前，按照上述条件欢迎所有新来者？美国工业已不再像曾经那样自由，美国企业已不再自由；资本微薄的人发现自己越来越难以涉足该领域，越来越不可能与巨头抗衡。原因何在？因为这个国家的法律未能阻止强者碾碎弱者。这就是原因所在；正因为强者碾碎了弱者，强者才得以主宰这个国家的工业与经济生活。无人能否认，努力奋斗的途径已变得愈发狭窄与僵化；任何了解这个国家工业发展的人都必定注意到，若不以与掌控国家工业的巨头联合为条件，大额信贷正变得越来越难以获取；任何人也必定注意到，凡是试图在已被庞大资本联盟控制的制造流程中展开竞争的人，不久就会发现自己要么被排挤出局，要么被迫出售资产任凭兼并。
在美国，有许许多多的事物亟待重建。

===Original===

I should like to take a census of the business men﻿—I mean the rank and file of the business men﻿—as to whether they think that business conditions in this country, or rather whether the organization of business in this country, is satisfactory or not. I know what they would say if they dared. If they could vote secretly they would vote overwhelmingly that the present organization of business was meant for the big fellows and was not meant for the little fellows; that it was meant for those who are at the top and was meant to exclude those who are at the bottom; that it was meant to shut out beginners, to prevent new entries in the race, to prevent the building up of competitive enterprises that would interfere with the monopolies which the great trusts have built up.
What this country needs above everything else is a body of laws which will look after the men who are on the make rather than the men who are already made. Because the men who are already made are not going to live indefinitely, and they are not always kind enough to leave sons as able and as honest as they are.
The originative part of America, the part of America that makes new enterprises, the part into which the ambitious and gifted workingman makes his way up, the class that saves, that plans, that organizes, that presently spreads its enterprises until they have a national scope and character﻿—that middle class is being more and more squeezed out by the processes which we have been taught to call processes of prosperity. Its members are sharing prosperity, no doubt; but what alarms me is that they are not *originating* prosperity. No country can afford to have its prosperity originated by a small controlling class. The treasury of America does not lie in the brains of the small body of men now in control of the great enterprises that have been concentrated under the direction of a very small number of persons. The treasury of America lies in those ambitions, those energies, that cannot be restricted to a special favored class. It depends upon the inventions of unknown men, upon the originations of unknown men, upon the ambitions of unknown men. Every country is renewed out of the ranks of the unknown, not out of the ranks of those already famous and powerful and in control.

===Chinese===

我很想对商界人士做一次普查——我指的是广大普通商界人士——看看他们认为这个国家的商业状况，或者说这个国家的商业组织结构，究竟是否令人满意。我知道如果他们敢于直言，他们会说些什么。如果能进行无记名投票，他们会以压倒性多数判定：现行的商业组织结构是为大巨头量身打造的，并非为小人物准备；它是为了位于顶层的人服务，旨在排斥处于底层的人；它的目的在于把初创者拒之门外，防止新竞争者加入赛道，阻止建立任何可能撼动巨型托拉斯所构筑之垄断（monopoly）的竞争性企业。
这个国家最为迫切需要的，是一套关照正在奋力打拼者（men who are on the make）而非已经功成名就者（men who are already made）的法律体系。因为那些功成名就的人不可能长生不老，而且他们并不总是仁慈地留下像他们一样能干且正直的子嗣。
美国的源发力量，美国创办新企业的那部分力量，那些雄心勃勃、富有天赋的劳工向上攀登所汇入的力量，那个储蓄、规划、组织并逐步将其企业拓展至具有全国规模与特征的阶级——正是这一中产阶级，正日益被我们被灌输称为繁荣（prosperity）进程的机制所挤压出局。毫无疑问，其成员也在分享繁荣；但令我警觉的是，他们不再是繁荣的“源发者”（originating prosperity）。没有任何国家承受得起由少数统治阶级来掌控繁荣的源头。美国的宝藏并不存在于如今掌控着在极少数人指挥下集中的大型企业的少数人脑中。美国的宝藏深藏于那些绝不能被局限于特定特权阶级的雄心与活力之中。它依赖于默默无闻之辈的发明，依赖于无名之辈的独创，依赖于芸芸众生的抱负。每个国家的生机皆焕发于默默无闻的大众行列，而非出自那些早已名声显赫、手握大权与控制权之人的圈子。

===Original===

There has come over the land that un-American set of conditions which enables a small number of men who control the government to get favors from the government; by those favors to exclude their fellows from equal business opportunity; by those favors to extend a network of control that will presently dominate every industry in the country, and so make men forget the ancient time when America lay in every hamlet, when America was to be seen in every fair valley, when America displayed her great forces on the broad prairies, ran her fine fires of enterprise up over the mountainsides and down into the bowels of the earth, and eager men were everywhere captains of industry, not employees; not looking to a distant city to find out what they might do, but looking about among their neighbors, finding credit according to their character, not according to their connections, finding credit in proportion to what was known to be in them and behind them, not in proportion to the securities they held that were approved where they were not known. In order to start an enterprise now, you have to be authenticated, in a perfectly impersonal way, not according to yourself, but according to what you own that somebody else approves of your owning. You cannot begin such an enterprise as those that have made America until you are so authenticated, until you have succeeded in obtaining the goodwill of large allied capitalists. Is that freedom? That is dependence, not freedom.
We used to think in the old-fashioned days when life was very simple that all that government had to do was to put on a policeman’s uniform, and say, “Now don’t anybody hurt anybody else.” We used to say that the ideal of government was for every man to be left alone and not interfered with, except when he interfered with somebody else; and that the best government was the government that did as little governing as possible. That was the idea that obtained in Jefferson’s time. But we are coming now to realize that life is so complicated that we are not dealing with the old conditions, and that the law has to step in and create new conditions under which we may live, the conditions which will make it tolerable for us to live.

===Chinese===

这片土地上出现了一种违背美国精神的状况，它使得控制政府的少数人能够从政府获取偏袒；凭借这些恩惠，他们将同胞排斥在平等的商业机会之外；凭借这些恩惠，他们构建起一张控制之网，不久便将主宰该国的每一个行业，从而使人们忘却了那个古老的时代——在那个时代，美国存在于每一个村落，呈现在每一座秀美山谷之中，辽阔的大草原展示着美国的雄厚伟力，进取精神的炽热火光燃遍山峦，深入地底，满怀热忱的人们处处皆是产业领袖，而非受雇之仆；他们无需遥望远方都市去探寻自己能做什么，而是环顾邻里，依据自身的品格而非人脉关系获得信贷，依据人们对其为人与背景的信赖而非他们在素不相识之处被认可的抵押资产来获取信贷。如今为了开创一番事业，你必须以一种完全非人格化的方式接受验证——不是依据你本人的品行，而是依据你拥有的资产是否得到他人的认可。在你获得这种资质认可之前，在你成功获得大型联盟资本家的青睐之前，你根本无法开启那些曾经造就了美国的事业。那是自由吗？那是依附，绝非自由。
我们过去在生活非常简单的旧时代总以为，政府所要做的不过是穿上警察制服，说一句：“现在谁也不许伤害谁。”我们过去常说，政府的理想状态是让每个人不受干扰、独处其乐，除非他侵犯了他人；最好的政府是管得最少的政府。那是托马斯·杰斐逊时代盛行的观念。但我们如今逐渐意识到，生活已变得如此错综复杂，我们面对的不再是过去的条件，法律必须介入并创造我们赖以生存的新条件，创造那些能使我们的生存得以维系的可忍受的条件。

===Original===

Let me illustrate what I mean: It used to be true in our cities that every family occupied a separate house of its own, that every family had its own little premises, that every family was separated in its life from every other family. That is no longer the case in our great cities. Families live in tenements, they live in flats, they live on floors; they are piled layer upon layer in the great tenement houses of our crowded districts, and not only are they piled layer upon layer, but they are associated room by room, so that there is in every room, sometimes, in our congested districts, a separate family. In some foreign countries they have made much more progress than we in handling these things. In the city of Glasgow, for example (Glasgow is one of the model cities of the world), they have made up their minds that the entries and the hallways of great tenements are public streets. Therefore, the policeman goes up the stairway, and patrols the corridors; the lighting department of the city sees to it that the halls are abundantly lighted. The city does not deceive itself into supposing that that great building is a unit from which the police are to keep out and the civic authority to be excluded, but it says: “These are public highways, and light is needed in them, and control by the authority of the city.”
I liken that to our great modern industrial enterprises. A corporation is very like a large tenement house; it isn’t the premises of a single commercial family; it is just as much a public affair as a tenement house is a network of public highways.
When you offer the securities of a great corporation to anybody who wishes to purchase them, you must open that corporation to the inspection of everybody who wants to purchase. There must, to follow out the figure of the tenement house, be lights along the corridors, there must be police patrolling the openings, there must be inspection wherever it is known that men may be deceived with regard to the contents of the premises. If we believe that fraud lies in wait for us, we must have the means of determining whether our suspicions are well founded or not. Similarly, the treatment of labor by the great corporations is not what it was in Jefferson’s time. Whenever bodies of men employ bodies of men, it ceases to be a private relationship.

===Chinese===

请允许我阐明我的意图：在过去的城市里，每个家庭往往拥有一所独立的居所，每个家庭有自己的小院落，每个家庭的生活都与其他家庭分隔开来。如今在我们的大城市里已不再是这般情景。家庭挤在公寓里、套房里、单层楼面里；在我们拥挤街区的巨型廉租公寓楼中，他们层层叠叠地聚居在一起；不仅如此，他们还一间挨着一间地挤在一起，以至于在拥挤街区的每一个房间里，有时都住着一户单独的家庭。在一些外国，他们在处理这些问题上取得了远比我们大的进步（progress）。例如在格拉斯哥市（Glasgow 是世界上的典范城市之一），他们认定大型廉租公寓楼的门厅和走廊属于公共街道。因此，警察巡查楼梯、巡视走廊；市政照明部门确保大厅灯火通明。该市并未自欺欺人地认为那座大楼是一个警察不得入内、市政当局应当被排除在外的私域单元，而是明确宣称：“这些是公共通道，其中需要照明，需要受到市政当局的监管。”
我将此比作我们现代的庞大工业企业。一家公司非常像一座大型廉租公寓楼；它不再属于单个商业家庭的私有领地；它就像廉租公寓楼是公共通道网络一样，完全是一项公共事务。
当你向任何有意认购者发售大公司的证券时，你必须将该公司向每一位有意购买者公开以供审查。若沿用廉租公寓楼的比喻，走廊沿途必须有照明，出入口必须有警察巡逻，凡是已知可能存在隐瞒欺骗的地方都必须接受检查。如果我们确信欺诈正潜伏待机，我们就必须拥有查明疑虑是否有真凭实据的手段。同样，大公司对待劳工的方式也不再等同于杰斐逊时代。每当人群组织雇用人群组织之时，这就绝不再是私人的关系。

===Original===

So that when courts hold that workingmen cannot peaceably dissuade other workingmen from taking employment, as was held in a notable case in New Jersey, they simply show that their minds and understandings are lingering in an age which has passed away. This dealing of great bodies of men with other bodies of men is a matter of public scrutiny, and should be a matter of public regulation.
Similarly, it was no business of the law in the time of Jefferson to come into my house and see how I kept house. But when my house, when my so-called private property, became a great mine, and men went along dark corridors amidst every kind of danger in order to dig out of the bowels of the earth things necessary for the industries of a whole nation, and when it came about that no individual owned these mines, that they were owned by great stock companies, then all the old analogies absolutely collapsed and it became the right of the government to go down into these mines to see whether human beings were properly treated in them or not; to see whether accidents were properly safeguarded against; to see whether modern economical methods of using these inestimable riches of the earth were followed or were not followed. If somebody puts a derrick improperly secured on top of a building or overtopping the street, then the government of the city has the right to see that that derrick is so secured that you and I can walk under it and not be afraid that the heavens are going to fall on us. Likewise, in these great beehives where in every corridor swarm men of flesh and blood, it is the privilege of the government, whether of the State or of the United States, as the case may be, to see that human life is protected, that human lungs have something to breathe.
These, again, are merely illustrations of conditions. We are in a new world, struggling under old laws. As we go inspecting our lives today, surveying this new scene of centralized and complex society, we shall find many more things out of joint.
---
One of the most alarming phenomena of the time﻿—or rather it would be alarming if the nation had not awakened to it and shown its determination to control it﻿—one of the most significant signs of the new social era is the degree to which government has become associated with business. I speak, for the moment, of the control over the government exercised by Big Business.

===Chinese===

因此，正如新泽西州（New Jersey）一桩著名的判例所判定的那样，当法院裁定工人不能和平劝阻其他工人接受雇佣时，他们不过是表明自己的思想与认识依然滞留在那个已经逝去的时代。庞大的人群组织与其他人群组织打交道，乃是关涉公众监督的事务，应当纳入公共监管的范畴。
同样，在杰斐逊时代，法律无权闯入我的私宅过问我如何料理家务。但是，当我的房屋、当我所谓的私有财产变成了一座巨大的矿山，工人们为了挖掘维系整个国家工业所必需的矿藏，在各种危险之中穿行于黑暗廊道；并且当这些矿山不再由任何个人拥有，而是由大型股份公司所有时，所有旧有的类比便彻底崩溃了，政府完全有权深入这些矿山，去调查其中的人是否得到了公正对待；去检查是否妥善防范了事故；去检查是否遵循了利用这些宝贵地球财富的现代经济方法。如果有人在建筑物顶上或悬在街道上方架设了固定不良的起重机，那么市政府就有权确保该起重机牢固安全，以使你我走在下面时无需担忧天崩地陷。同样，在这些每个走廊里都挤满有血有肉的人群的巨大蜂巢中，无论是州政府还是美国（United States）联邦政府，视具体情况而定，其特权（privilege）与职责都在于保障人类生命免受危害，保障人类的肺部有空气可吸。
这些同样不过是对现状的说明而已。我们生活在一个新世界里，却在旧法律的束缚下苦苦挣扎。当我们今天审视自己的生活，打量这个高度中心化与复杂的社会新图景时，我们会发现更多脱节与错位的事物。
---
当前最令人警惕的现象之一——或者更确切地说，如果国家尚未觉醒并展现出控制它的决心，那确实是令人警惕的——也是新社会时代最显著的标志之一，就是政府与商业紧密结合的程度。此刻我所谈论的，正是大企业对政府施加的控制。

===Original===

Behind the whole subject, of course, is the truth that, in the new order, government and business must be associated closely. But that association is at present of a nature absolutely intolerable; the precedence is wrong, the association is upside down. Our government has been for the past few years under the control of heads of great allied corporations with special interests. It has not controlled these interests and assigned them a proper place in the whole system of business; it has submitted itself to their control. As a result, there have grown up vicious systems and schemes of governmental favoritism (the most obvious being the extravagant tariff), far-reaching in effect upon the whole fabric of life, touching to his injury every inhabitant of the land, laying unfair and impossible handicaps upon competitors, imposing taxes in every direction, stifling everywhere the free spirit of American enterprise.
Now this has come about naturally; as we go on we shall see how very naturally. It is no use denouncing anybody, or anything, except human nature. Nevertheless, it is an intolerable thing that the government of the republic should have got so far out of the hands of the people; should have been captured by interests which are special and not general. In the train of this capture follow the troops of scandals, wrongs, indecencies, with which our politics swarm.
There are cities in America of whose government we are ashamed. There are cities everywhere, in every part of the land, in which we feel that, not the interests of the public, but the interests of special privileges, of selfish men, are served; where contracts take precedence over public interest. Not only in big cities is this the case. Have you not noticed the growth of socialistic sentiment in the smaller towns? Not many months ago I stopped at a little town in Nebraska, and while my train lingered I met on the platform a very engaging young fellow dressed in overalls who introduced himself to me as the mayor of the town, and added that he was a Socialist. I said, “What does that mean? Does that mean that this town is socialistic?” “No, sir,” he said; “I have not deceived myself; the vote by which I was elected was about 20 percent socialistic and 80 percent protest.” It was protest against the treachery to the people of those who led both the other parties of that town.

===Chinese===

在整个议题的背后，当然存在着这样一个事实：在新的秩序下，政府与商业必须紧密关联。但这种关联在目前展现出一种绝对难以容忍的性质：主次颠倒，关系本末倒置。过去几年里，我们的政府一直处于具有特殊利益的大型联合公司首脑的控制之下。政府不仅没有控制这些利益集团（the interests）并赋予其在整个商业体系中适当的地位，反而屈服于它们的控制。其结果是滋生了恶性的政府偏袒制度与方案（最显而易见的便是高昂的关税），对整个生活体系产生了深远影响，损害了这片土地上的每一位居民，给竞争对手设置了不公平且难以逾越的障碍，四处征税，处处扼杀了美国企业自由进取的精神。
这一局面的形成本属自然；随着论述深入，我们将看到它是如何自然而然发生的。谴责任何人或任何事物都是徒劳的，除非谴责人性本身。然而，共和国的政府竟然如此严重地脱离人民（the people）的掌控，竟然被特殊而非普遍的利益集团所俘获，这是不可容忍的事情。伴随这种俘获而来的，是充斥于我们政治生活中的成群丑闻、不公与卑劣行径。
在美国，有些城市的市政令我们深感蒙羞。在全国各地的许多城市中，我们都能感受到，受到服务的不是公众利益，而是特殊特权（privilege）以及自私自利者的利益；在那里，商业合同凌驾于公共利益之上。不仅大城市如此，难道你们没有注意到在较小城镇中社会主义思潮的抬头吗？就在几个月前，我在内布拉斯加州的一个小镇短暂停留，在列车逗留期间，我在站台上遇到了一位穿着工装裤的非常迷人的年轻人，他向我自我介绍是该镇的镇长，并补充说自己是一名社会主义者。我问：“这意味着什么？这是否意味着这个城镇是信奉社会主义的？”“不，先生，”他说；“我并未自欺欺人；选举我当选的选票中，大约20%（20 percent）是出于社会主义诉求，而80%（80 percent）则是出于抗议。”那是对该镇另外两党领导人背叛人民行径的愤怒抗议。

===Original===

All over the Union people are coming to feel that they have no control over the course of affairs. I live in one of the greatest States in the union, which was at one time in slavery. Until two years ago we had witnessed with increasing concern the growth in New Jersey of a spirit of almost cynical despair. Men said: “We vote; we are offered the platform we want; we elect the men who stand on that platform, and we get absolutely nothing.” So they began to ask: “What is the use of voting? We know that the machines of both parties are subsidized by the same persons, and therefore it is useless to turn in either direction.”
This is not confined to some of the state governments and those of some of the towns and cities. We know that something intervenes between the people of the United States and the control of their own affairs at Washington. It is not the people who have been ruling there of late.
Why are we in the presence, why are we at the threshold, of a revolution? Because we are profoundly disturbed by the influences which we see reigning in the determination of our public life and our public policy. There was a time when America was blithe with self-confidence. She boasted that she, and she alone, knew the processes of popular government; but now she sees her sky overcast; she sees that there are at work forces which she did not dream of in her hopeful youth.
Don’t you know that some man with eloquent tongue, without conscience, who did not care for the nation, could put this whole country into a flame? Don’t you know that this country from one end to the other believes that something is wrong? What an opportunity it would be for some man without conscience to spring up and say: “This is the way. Follow me!”﻿—and lead in paths of destruction!
The old order changeth﻿—changeth under our very eyes, not quietly and equably, but swiftly and with the noise and heat and tumult of reconstruction.
I suppose that all struggle for law has been conscious, that very little of it has been blind or merely instinctive. It is the fashion to say, as if with superior knowledge of affairs and of human weakness, that every age has been an age of transition, and that no age is more full of change than another; yet in very few ages of the world can the struggle for change have been so widespread, so deliberate, or upon so great a scale as in this in which we are taking part.

===Chinese===

整个联邦的人民都逐渐感受到，他们无法掌控事态的发展。我生活在联邦中最大的州之一，那个州曾一度处于奴隶制之下。直到2年前（two years ago），我们在新泽西州（New Jersey）日益担忧地目睹了一种近乎冷嘲热讽的绝望情绪在滋长。人们说：“我们去投票；我们获得了合意的竞选纲领（platform）；我们选出了支持该纲领的人，可我们最终什么都没有得到。”于是他们开始追问：“投票有什么用？我们深知两党的政治机器都受同一伙人的资助，因此倒向任何一方都无济于事。”
这种情况并不局限于某些州政府以及一些城镇和城市。我们知道，在美利坚合众国的人民与他们在华盛顿（Washington）对自身事务的掌控之间，横亘着某种阻隔。近来在那里执政的并不是人民。
为什么我们身处一场革命的面前，为什么我们站在革命的门槛之上？因为我们深切不安地看到那些在决定我们的公共生活和公共政策中占据支配地位的影响力。曾几何时，美国沉浸在无忧无虑的自信之中，夸耀自己而且唯有自己通晓平民政府的运作法则；但如今她看到自己的天空阴云密布；她看到一些在她充满希望的青年时代未曾梦想过的力量正在发挥作用。
难道你们不知道，某个巧舌如簧、丧尽天良、毫不在乎国家利益之徒，足以让整个国家陷入一片火海吗？难道你们不知道，这个国家从这一端到那一端都坚信某些事情出了差错吗？对于某些丧尽良知之辈而言，突然跳出来喊道“这就是出路，跟我来吧！”，并将其引向毁灭之路，将是何等天赐良机！
旧秩序正在改变（The old order changeth）——就在我们眼前发生改变，不是平静安稳地改变，而是伴随着重建的喧嚣、热烈与动荡迅疾地发生改变。
我认为，所有为法律而战的斗争都带有自觉意识，其中极少是盲目或纯粹出于本能的。人们习惯于装作对世事和人性弱点了如指掌的样子说：每个时代都是过渡时代，没有任何时代比其他时代充满了更多变革；然而，在世界历史的极少时代里，谋求变革的斗争能够像我们亲身参与的这场斗争一样如此广泛、如此深思熟虑、或者具备如此巨大的规模。

===Original===

The transition we are witnessing is no equable transition of growth and normal alteration; no silent, unconscious unfolding of one age into another, its natural heir and successor. Society is looking itself over, in our day, from top to bottom; is making fresh and critical analysis of its very elements; is questioning its oldest practices as freely as its newest, scrutinizing every arrangement and motive of its life; and it stands ready to attempt nothing less than a radical reconstruction, which only frank and honest counsels and the forces of generous cooperation can hold back from becoming a revolution. We are in a temper to reconstruct economic society, as we were once in a temper to reconstruct political society, and political society may itself undergo a radical modification in the process. I doubt if any age was ever more conscious of its task or more unanimously desirous of radical and extended changes in its economic and political practice.
We stand in the presence of a revolution﻿—not a bloody revolution; America is not given to the spilling of blood﻿—but a silent revolution, whereby America will insist upon recovering in practice those ideals which she has always professed, upon securing a government devoted to the general interest and not to special interests.
We are upon the eve of a great reconstruction. It calls for creative statesmanship as no age has done since that great age in which we set up the government under which we live, that government which was the admiration of the world until it suffered wrongs to grow up under it which have made many of our own compatriots question the freedom of our institutions and preach revolution against them. I do not fear revolution. I have unshaken faith in the power of America to keep its self-possession. Revolution will come in peaceful guise, as it came when we put aside the crude government of the Confederation and created the great Federal Union which governs individuals, not States, and which has been these hundred and thirty years our vehicle of progress. Some radical changes we must make in our law and practice. Some reconstructions we must push forward, which a new age and new circumstances impose upon us. But we can do it all in calm and sober fashion, like statesmen and patriots.
I do not speak of these things in apprehension, because all is open and aboveboard.

===Chinese===

我们所目睹的过渡，绝非生长与常规演变的平缓过渡；绝非一个时代悄无声息、不知觉地展现在作为其天然继承者的另一时代面前。在我们的时代，社会正从上到下自我反思；正在对其基本构成要素进行全新且批判性的剖析；正在毫无顾忌地对其最古老的惯例与最新颖的实践提出质疑，审视其生活的每一项安排与动机；并且它准备尝试进行一场不折不扣的根本性重建，只有坦诚而正直的劝告以及慷慨合作的力量，才能阻止这场重建演变为一场暴力革命。我们正处于重建经济社会的氛围之中，正如我们曾经处于重建政治社会的氛围之中一样，而在这一进程中，政治社会本身也可能经历根本性的调整。我怀疑，是否曾有任何时代比当前时代更清醒地意识到自身的使命，或者更一致地渴望在其经济与政治实践中实现根本而深远的变革。
我们站在一场革命的面前——这不是一场流血的革命；美国不倾向于流血——而是一场无声的革命，在这场革命中，美国将坚持在实践中重拾她一贯宣扬的理想，确保建立一个致力于普遍利益而非特殊利益的政府。
我们正处于伟大重建的前夜。它呼唤着开创性的治国理政才能，自我们建立起赖以生存的政府那个伟大时代以来，没有任何时代像今天这样需要这种才能。那个政府曾令全世界钦佩，直到它任由弊端滋生蔓延，以至于使得我们许多同胞怀疑我们制度的自由，并鼓吹推翻这些制度的革命。我并不惧怕革命。我坚信美国具有保持自我克制的力量。革命将以和平的姿态到来，正如我们当年摒弃松散粗糙的邦联政府，创立管辖个人而非管辖各州的伟大联邦合众国时一样；而在过去的130年（hundred and thirty years）里，这个联邦正是我们进步（progress）的载体。我们必须在法律与实践中做出一些根本性的改变。我们必须推进某些由新时代和新环境强加于我们的重建。但我们完全能够像政治家和爱国者那样，以平静而沉着的方式完成这一切。
我谈及这些并非出于忧虑，因为一切都是公开且光明正大的。

===Original===

This is not a day in which great forces rally in secret. The whole stupendous program must be publicly planned and canvassed. Good temper, the wisdom that comes of sober counsel, the energy of thoughtful and unselfish men, the habit of cooperation and of compromise which has been bred in us by long years of free government, in which reason rather than passion has been made to prevail by the sheer virtue of candid and universal debate, will enable us to win through to still another great age without violence.

===Chinese===

当今时代已不再是大势力在暗中集结的时代。整个宏伟的计划必须公开谋划并进行广泛探讨。良好的心态、来自深思熟虑的明智建言、深谋远虑且大公无私者的充沛活力，以及在多年自由政府熏陶下培养起来的合作与妥协习惯——在那种政府体制下，纯粹凭借坦率与普遍的辩论，理性而非激情得以占据上风——将使我们能够毫无暴力地迈向又一个伟大的时代。

## II What Is Progress? / 何谓进步
===Original===

In that sage and veracious chronicle, *Alice Through the Looking-Glass*, it is recounted how, on a noteworthy occasion, the little heroine is seized by the Red Chess Queen, who races her off at a terrific pace. They run until both of them are out of breath; then they stop, and Alice looks around her and says, “Why, we are just where we were when we started!” “Oh, yes,” says the Red Queen; “you have to run twice as fast as that to get anywhere else.”
That is a parable of progress. The laws of this country have not kept up with the change of economic circumstances in this country; they have not kept up with the change of political circumstances; and therefore we are not even where we were when we started. We shall have to run, not until we are out of breath, but until we have caught up with our own conditions, before we shall be where we were when we started; when we started this great experiment which has been the hope and the beacon of the world. And we should have to run twice as fast as any rational program I have seen in order to get anywhere else.
I am, therefore, forced to be a progressive, if for no other reason, because we have not kept up with our changes of conditions, either in the economic field or in the political field. We have not kept up as well as other nations have. We have not kept our practices adjusted to the facts of the case, and until we do, and unless we do, the facts of the case will always have the better of the argument; because if you do not adjust your laws to the facts, so much the worse for the laws, not for the facts, because law trails along after the facts. Only that law is unsafe which runs ahead of the facts and beckons to it and makes it follow the will-o’-the-wisps of imaginative projects.
Business is in a situation in America which it was never in before; it is in a situation to which we have not adjusted our laws. Our laws are still meant for business done by individuals; they have not been satisfactorily adjusted to business done by great combinations, and we have got to adjust them. I do not say we may or may not; I say we must; there is no choice. If your laws do not fit your facts, the facts are not injured, the law is damaged; because the law, unless I have studied it amiss, is the expression of the facts in legal relationships.

===Chinese===

在那部充满哲理且真切生动的名著《爱丽丝镜中奇遇记》（Alice Through the Looking-Glass）中，记述了这样一个引人注目的场景：小女主角被红棋王后抓入院落，以惊人的速度飞奔。她们一直跑，直到双双喘不过气来才停下；此时爱丽丝环顾四周说道：“哎呀，我们怎么还在出发时的地方！”红王后答道：“噢，正是如此！要想到别的地方去，你至少得跑得比这快上一倍才行。”
这正是进步（progress）的一则寓言。我们国家的法律未能跟上本国经济环境的变迁，也未能跟上政治环境的演变；因此我们甚至连当初起跑时的位置都未能保住。我们必须奔跑，不是跑到气喘吁吁，而是必须奔跑直至赶上我们自身的现实条件，然后才能重回我们出发时的起点——回到我们开启那场曾作为世界希望与灯塔的伟大实验的起点。而且，要想抵达任何新的境地，我们奔跑的速度必须比我所见过的任何理性规划都要快上一倍。
因此，倘若没有其他理由，单凭我们未能跟上自身经济与政治领域条件的变迁这一事实，就迫使我必须成为一名进步主义者。我们跟进步伐的成效不如其他国家。我们未能使自己的实践适应客观现实，在做到这一点之前，除非我们做到这一点，客观现实在辩论中总会占据上风；因为如果你不让法律去适应现实，受损的只会是法律而非现实，毕竟法律总是尾随现实之后。唯有那种凌驾于现实之上、向现实招手并迫使其追逐虚妄幻想工程的法律，才是危险不可靠的。
美国的商业正处于前所未有的境遇之中；我们尚未使法律适应这种境遇。我们的法律依然是为个体开展的商业活动量身定制的；它们尚未妥善适应庞大联合体所开展的商业运作，而我们必须对其加以调整。我不是说我们可以调整也可以不调整，我认为我们必须调整，别无选择。如果你的法律不能契合现实，现实毫发无损，受损的却是法律；因为除非我把法学研究彻底弄错了，否则法律本质上就是现实在法律关系中的体现。

===Original===

Laws have never altered the facts; laws have always necessarily expressed the facts; adjusted interests as they have arisen and have changed toward one another.
Politics in America is in a case which sadly requires attention. The system set up by our law and our usage doesn’t work﻿—or at least it can’t be depended on; it is made to work only by a most unreasonable expenditure of labor and pains. The government, which was designed for the people, has got into the hands of bosses and their employers, the special interests. An invisible empire has been set up above the forms of democracy.
There are serious things to do. Does any man doubt the great discontent in this country? Does any man doubt that there are grounds and justifications for discontent? Do we dare stand still? Within the past few months we have witnessed (along with other strange political phenomena, eloquently significant of popular uneasiness) on one side a doubling of the Socialist vote and on the other the posting on dead walls and hoardings all over the country of certain very attractive and diverting bills warning citizens that it was “better to be safe than sorry” and advising them to “let well enough alone.” Apparently a good many citizens doubted whether the situation they were advised to let alone was really well enough, and concluded that they would take a chance of being sorry. To me, these counsels of do-nothingism, these counsels of sitting still for fear something would happen, these counsels addressed to the hopeful, energetic people of the United States, telling them that they are not wise enough to touch their own affairs without marring them, constitute the most extraordinary argument of fatuous ignorance I ever heard. Americans are not yet cowards. True, their self-reliance has been sapped by years of submission to the doctrine that prosperity is something that benevolent magnates provide for them with the aid of the government; their self-reliance has been weakened, but not so utterly destroyed that you can twit them about it. The American people are not naturally stand-patters. Progress is the word that charms their ears and stirs their hearts.
There are, of course, Americans who have not yet heard that anything is going on. The circus might come to town, have the big parade and go, without their catching a sight of the camels or a note of the calliope.

===Chinese===

法律从未改变过客观现实；法律向来必然是在表达现实，调节着因彼此产生与变化而形成的各种利益关系。
美国的政治正处于一个令人痛心、亟待关注的境地。由我们的法律与惯例所建立的体制无法正常运转——或者说至少无法令人信服地依靠；它只有在付出极其不合理的劳力与痛苦的代价下才能维系运转。原本为人民（the people）设计的政府，如今落入了政治头目（bosses）及其雇主——即特殊利益集团手中。在民主的形式之上，建立起了一个隐形帝国。
我们有极其严肃的任务亟待完成。有谁会怀疑这个国家所弥漫的巨大不满吗？有谁会怀疑这种不满拥有充分的理由与正当性吗？我们难道敢止步不前吗？在过去的几个月里，我们目睹了（伴随着其他生动表明大众普遍不安的奇异政治现象）一方面社会主义选票翻倍，另一方面全国各地的残垣断壁和广告牌上张贴出某些极具诱惑力且转移视线的海报，告诫公民“安全胜于后悔”，奉劝大家“维持现状为妙”。显然，相当多的公民怀疑奉劝他们保持现状的局面是否真的足够好，并决意冒一次后悔的风险。在我看来，这些无所作为的主张，这些因害怕出事而主张按兵不动的建言，这些向美利坚合众国满怀希望、精力充沛的人民发出的、妄称他们不够聪明以至于触碰自身事务只会把事情弄糟的告诫，构成了我所听过的最荒唐无知的论调。美国人尚未沦为懦夫。诚然，多年来屈从于“繁荣（prosperity）是由仁慈的大亨在政府协助下提供给大众”的信条，确实侵蚀了他们的自立精神；他们的自立精神固然有所减弱，但绝未彻底泯灭到任由别人嘲讽的地步。美国人民生来就不是墨守成规者（stand-patters）。“进步”正是那个令他们侧耳倾听、心潮澎湃的词汇。
当然，有些美国人确实尚未听说有任何事情正在发生。即便马戏团来到镇上，举行了盛大的游行然后离开，他们也可能连骆驼的影子都没看到，连汽笛风琴的一声乐响都没听见。

===Original===

There are people, even Americans, who never move themselves or know that anything else is moving.
A friend of mine who had heard of the Florida “cracker,” as they call a certain ne’er-do-weel portion of the population down there, when passing through the State in a train, asked someone to point out a “cracker” to him. The man asked replied, “Well, if you see something off in the woods that looks brown, like a stump, you will know it is either a stump or a cracker; if it moves, it is a stump.”
Now, movement has no virtue in itself. Change is not worth while for its own sake. I am not one of those who love variety for its own sake. If a thing is good today, I should like to have it stay that way tomorrow. Most of our calculations in life are dependent upon things staying the way they are. For example, if, when you got up this morning, you had forgotten how to dress, if you had forgotten all about those ordinary things which you do almost automatically, which you can almost do half awake, you would have to find out what you did yesterday. I am told by the psychologists that if I did not remember who I was yesterday, I should not know who I am today, and that, therefore, my very identity depends upon my being able to tally today with yesterday. If they do not tally, then I am confused; I do not know who I am, and I have to go around and ask somebody to tell me my name and where I came from.
I am not one of those who wish to break connection with the past; I am not one of those who wish to change for the mere sake of variety. The only men who do that are the men who want to forget something, the men who filled yesterday with something they would rather not recollect today, and so go about seeking diversion, seeking abstraction in something that will blot out recollection, or seeking to put something into them which will blot out all recollection. Change is not worth while unless it is improvement. If I move out of my present house because I do not like it, then I have got to choose a better house, or build a better house, to justify the change.
It would seem a waste of time to point out that ancient distinction﻿—between mere change and improvement. Yet there is a class of mind that is prone to confuse them.

===Chinese===

确实有些人，甚至包括某些美国人，自己从不活动，也不知道有其他任何事物在变迁。我的一位在新泽西州（New Jersey）乡村居住了很长时间的朋友告诉我，那里有一个人每天早晨坐在村头商店门前的一张长凳上，整天坐在那里直到晚上九点钟商店打烊。有一天，我的朋友走到他面前问他：“先生，我想请教一下，你整天坐在这里都干些什么呢？”他说：“好吧，有时候我坐着思考，有时候我只是干坐着。”在这个国家里，坐着思考的人是少数，而只是干坐着的人则大有人在。
在这个世界上，最令人欣慰的事情之一莫过于：在人生漫长的旅途之中，一个人总能邂逅那些正在思考的人，并与他们为伴。与一位头脑开窍、思想活跃的人交谈五分钟，胜过整天在街上闲逛一无所获。然而，许多人由于未曾思考，因而对事物正在发生变化感到震惊与恐惧。如果某件事在某处发生了哪怕片刻的移动，他们便会惊慌失措地认定整个世界的运转都失常了。他们自称保守主义者。然而在很多情况下，他们根本不是保守主义者，不过是反动分子罢了；因为每当事物开始运动时，他们不仅拒绝跟随前进，反而一心只想倒退回它们起步之前的位置。
政界充斥着这种思维方式的人。但我必须向大家说明，这种思维方式并不专属于保守主义者。我们曾有过一些政治领袖，他们对伟大的理解就是必须永远狂热地忙碌个不停——至于做什么并不重要，只要忙碌就行；只要有动静，只要满尘飞扬，只要喧嚣震天，只要有疾驰奔走，他们就心满意足。

===Original===

We have had political leaders whose conception of greatness was to be forever frantically doing something﻿—it mattered little what; restless, vociferous men, without sense of the energy of concentration, knowing only the energy of succession. Now, life does not consist of eternally running to a fire. There is no virtue in going anywhere unless you will gain something by being there. The direction is just as important as the impetus of motion.
All progress depends on how fast you are going, and where you are going, and I fear there has been too much of this thing of knowing neither how fast we were going or where we were going. I have my private belief that we have been doing most of our progressiveness after the fashion of those things that in my boyhood days we called “treadmills,”﻿—a treadmill being a moving platform, with cleats on it, on which some poor devil of a mule was forced to walk forever without getting anywhere. Elephants and even other animals have been known to turn treadmills, making a good deal of noise, and causing certain wheels to go round, and I daresay grinding out some sort of product for somebody, but without achieving much progress. Lately, in an effort to persuade the elephant to move, really, his friends tried dynamite. It moved﻿—in separate and scattered parts, but it moved.
A cynical but witty Englishman said, in a book, not long ago, that it was a mistake to say of a conspicuously successful man, eminent in his line of business, that you could not bribe a man like that, because, he said, the point about such men is that they have been bribed﻿—not in the ordinary meaning of that word, not in any gross, corrupt sense, but they have achieved their great success by means of the existing order of things and therefore they have been put under bonds to see that that existing order of things is not changed; they are bribed to maintain the status quo.
It was for that reason that I used to say, when I had to do with the administration of an educational institution, that I should like to make the young gentlemen of the rising generation as unlike their fathers as possible.

===Chinese===

但这并非进步，因为进步并非仅仅是运动而已。我们必须知道自己奔向何方。在前进的道路上，我们必须有方向感。那种一味盲目的狂热行动，其危险程度绝不亚于仅仅坐着无所事事。
因此，在我们谈论进步时，必须澄清我们究竟指的是什么。我所理解的进步，绝非脱离现实基础的狂想，亦非盲目破坏过去的一切。然而，那些既得利益者——那些在现有秩序下聚敛了巨额财富、掌握了特权的人——往往最惧怕变革。他们被现有秩序的利益所收买，他们立下契约以确保现存体制不被更改；他们被收买以维护现状。
正是由于这个缘故，我过去在参与教育机构管理时经常说：我希望能使正在成长中的新一代年轻绅士们，尽可能地不同于他们的父辈。

===Original===

Not because their fathers lacked character or intelligence or knowledge or patriotism, but because their fathers, by reason of their advancing years and their established position in society, had lost touch with the processes of life; they had forgotten what it was to begin; they had forgotten what it was to rise; they had forgotten what it was to be dominated by the circumstances of their life on their way up from the bottom to the top, and, therefore, they were out of sympathy with the creative, formative and progressive forces of society.
Progress! Did you ever reflect that that word is almost a new one? No word comes more often or more naturally to the lips of modern man, as if the thing it stands for were almost synonymous with life itself, and yet men through many thousand years never talked or thought of progress. They thought in the other direction. Their stories of heroisms and glory were tales of the past. The ancestor wore the heavier armor and carried the larger spear. “There were giants in those days.” Now all that has altered. We think of the future, not the past, as the more glorious time in comparison with which the present is nothing. Progress, development﻿—those are modern words. The modern idea is to leave the past and press onward to something new.
But what is progress going to do with the past, and with the present? How is it going to treat them? With ignominy, or respect? Should it break with them altogether, or rise out of them, with its roots still deep in the older time? What attitude shall progressives take toward the existing order, toward those institutions of conservatism, the Constitution, the laws, and the courts?
Are those thoughtful men who fear that we are now about to disturb the ancient foundations of our institutions justified in their fear? If they are, we ought to go very slowly about the processes of change. If it is indeed true that we have grown tired of the institutions which we have so carefully and sedulously built up, then we ought to go very slowly and very carefully about the very dangerous task of altering them. We ought, therefore, to ask ourselves, first of all, whether thought in this country is tending to do anything by which we shall retrace our steps, or by which we shall change the whole direction of our development?

===Chinese===

这并非因为他们的父辈缺乏品格、智慧、学识或爱国情操，而是因为他们的父辈随着年事已高、在社会中地位稳固，已经脱离了生机勃勃的生活进程；他们已经遗忘了当初是如何白手起家，遗忘了当初是如何奋力攀登，遗忘了在从社会底层迈向顶峰的攀升道路上，受制于生存境遇究竟是何种滋味；因此，他们对社会中富有创造性、塑造性与进步性的力量缺乏同理心。
进步！你是否曾深思过，“进步”这个词其实几乎是个新概念？对于现代人而言，再没有哪个词比它更频繁、更自然地脱口而出了，仿佛它所代表的内涵几乎与生命本身同义；然而在长达数千年的岁月里，人类从未谈论或思索过进步。他们思考的方向恰恰相反。他们的英雄传说与辉煌史诗全都是过去的故事。先祖披挂更沉重的铠甲，手握更硕大的长矛。“在那个时代曾有巨人存在。”如今，这一切都彻底改变了。我们向往的是未来，而非过去；我们视未来为更加荣耀的时代，与之相比，现在显得微不足道。进步、发展——这些都是现代的字眼。现代的理念正是告别过去，奋勇迈向新生。
但是，进步将如何对待过去，又将如何对待当下？它将以何种态度对待它们？是以鄙弃，还是以尊重？它是应当与它们彻底决裂，还是在根植于旧时代的深厚土壤中拔地而起？进步主义者对于现存秩序、对于宪法（the Constitution）、法律和法院这些保守主义体制，究竟应当持何种态度？
那些担忧我们即将撼动体制之古老根基的有识之士，其担忧是否事出有因？倘若确有道理，我们在变革的进程中就应当极其审慎稳妥。如果我们确实对自己苦心孤诣、孜孜不倦建立起来的制度感到厌倦，那么在改动这些制度的极其危险的使命中，我们理应极为审慎、极为周详。因此，我们首先应当扪心自问：这个国家的思潮是否倾向于做出任何让我们重蹈覆辙之举，或者是否会彻底改变我们发展的整体方向？

===Original===

I believe, for one, that you cannot tear up ancient rootages and safely plant the tree of liberty in soil which is not native to it. I believe that the ancient traditions of a people are its ballast; you cannot make a tabula rasa upon which to write a political program. You cannot take a new sheet of paper and determine what your life shall be tomorrow. You must knit the new into the old. You cannot put a new patch on an old garment without ruining it; it must be not a patch, but something woven into the old fabric, of practically the same pattern, of the same texture and intention. If I did not believe that to be progressive was to preserve the essentials of our institutions, I for one could not be a progressive.
---
One of the chief benefits I used to derive from being president of a university was that I had the pleasure of entertaining thoughtful men from all over the world. I cannot tell you how much has dropped into my granary by their presence. I had been casting around in my mind for something by which to draw several parts of my political thought together when it was my good fortune to entertain a very interesting Scotsman who had been devoting himself to the philosophical thought of the seventeenth century. His talk was so engaging that it was delightful to hear him speak of anything, and presently there came out of the unexpected region of his thought the thing I had been waiting for. He called my attention to the fact that in every generation all sorts of speculation and thinking tend to fall under the formula of the dominant thought of the age. For example, after the Newtonian Theory of the universe had been developed, almost all thinking tended to express itself in the analogies of the Newtonian Theory, and since the Darwinian Theory has reigned amongst us, everybody is likely to express whatever he wishes to expound in terms of development and accommodation to environment.
Now, it came to me, as this interesting man talked, that the Constitution of the United States had been made under the dominion of the Newtonian Theory. You have only to read the papers of *The Federalist* to see that fact written on every page.

===Chinese===

我个人坚信，一个人绝不能连根拔起古老的根基，还妄图把自由之树安全地移植到非其原生的土壤之中。我深信，一个民族的古老传统是其稳定前行的压舱石；你绝不能把政治纲领写在一张白纸（tabula rasa）之上。你无法拿起一张崭新的白纸，便规定自己明天的生活应当如何。你必须将新生事物编织进旧有脉络之中。你绝不能在一件旧衣上打上一块新补丁而不同时毁掉它；它必须不是一块拼凑的补丁，而是织入旧布料中的经纬，具有基本相同的图案、质地与意图。如果我不相信成为进步主义者意味着捍卫我们制度的精髓，我个人就绝不可能成为一名进步主义者。
---
我过去担任大学校长时获得的主要益处之一，便是能够荣幸地款待来自世界各地的深思之士。我无法向你们言尽，他们的到访给我的思想谷仓增添了多么丰硕的积淀。我原本一直在脑海中苦苦寻觅某种能将我政治思想的各个部分融会贯通的纽带，正在此时，我有幸款待了一位非常有趣的苏格兰学者，他一直专注于十七世纪的哲学思想研究。他的谈吐极其引人引人入胜，听他谈论任何事物都是一种莫大的享受；未几，从他意想不到的思想领域中，浮现出了我一直苦苦期盼的东西。他提醒我注意这样一个事实：在每一个时代，各类思辨与思潮往往都会归入那个时代主流思想的范式之下。例如，在牛顿宇宙理论建立之后，几乎所有的思想都倾向于借用牛顿理论的类比来表达自身；而自从达尔文理论在我们之中确立支配地位以来，每个人都习惯于用发展以及对环境的适应来阐述自己想要阐发的任何理论。
当这位博学风趣的人士畅谈之时，我猛然领悟到：美利坚合众国的宪法（the Constitution）正是在牛顿理论的主导下制定出来的。你只需阅读《联邦党人文集》（The Federalist）的篇章，便会发现这一事实跃然于每一页纸上。

===Original===

They speak of the “checks and balances” of the Constitution, and use to express their idea the simile of the organization of the universe, and particularly of the solar system﻿—how by the attraction of gravitation the various parts are held in their orbits; and then they proceed to represent Congress, the Judiciary, and the President as a sort of imitation of the solar system.
They were only following the English Whigs, who gave Great Britain its modern constitution. Not that those Englishmen analyzed the matter, or had any theory about it; Englishmen care little for theories. It was a Frenchman, Montesquieu, who pointed out to them how faithfully they had copied Newton’s description of the mechanism of the heavens.
The makers of our Federal Constitution read Montesquieu with true scientific enthusiasm. They were scientists in their way﻿—the best way of their age﻿—those fathers of the nation. Jefferson wrote of “the laws of Nature,”﻿—and then by way of afterthought﻿—“and of Nature’s God.” And they constructed a government as they would have constructed an orrery﻿—to display the laws of nature. Politics in their thought was a variety of mechanics. The Constitution was founded on the law of gravitation. The government was to exist and move by virtue of the efficacy of “checks and balances.”
The trouble with the theory is that government is not a machine, but a living thing. It falls, not under the theory of the universe, but under the theory of organic life. It is accountable to Darwin, not to Newton. It is modified by its environment, necessitated by its tasks, shaped to its functions by the sheer pressure of life. No living thing can have its organs offset against each other, as checks, and live. On the contrary, its life is dependent upon their quick cooperation, their ready response to the commands of instinct or intelligence, their amicable community of purpose. Government is not a body of blind forces; it is a body of men, with highly differentiated functions, no doubt, in our modern day, of specialization, with a common task and purpose. Their cooperation is indispensable, their warfare fatal. There can be no successful government without the intimate, instinctive coordination of the organs of life and action. This is not theory, but fact, and displays its force as fact, whatever theories may be thrown across its track.

===Chinese===

他们谈论宪法的“权力制衡”（checks and balances），并借用宇宙组织、特别是太阳系的类比来表达其构想——说明各个组成部分是如何通过引力相互吸引而保持在各自的轨道上运行；进而他们将国会（Congress）、司法机构和总统描绘成对太阳系某种机制的模仿。
他们不过是在效仿英国辉格党人，正是后者赋予了大不列颠现代宪政体制。倒不是那些英国人对此进行了系统分析或提出了什么理论；英国人向来不太热衷于理论。是一位法国人——孟德斯鸠，向他们指出了他们是如何忠实地照搬了牛顿对天体运行机制的描述。
我们联邦宪法的缔造者怀着真正的科学热情研读了孟德斯鸠的著作。那些国父们以他们自己的方式——也是他们所处时代最优秀的方式——成为了科学家。杰斐逊写下了“自然法则”，随后又补充了“及自然法则之神”。他们构筑政府的方式，犹如制造一台展示天体运行仪（orrery）——用以彰显自然法则。在他们的思想中，政治乃是一种机械力学。宪法奠基于万有引力定律之上。政府的生存与运转，全然有赖于“权力制衡”（checks and balances）的效能。
这种理论的症结在于：政府绝非机械，而是一个活生生的有机体。它不应归属于天体宇宙理论，而应归属于有机生命理论。它应当向达尔文负责，而非向牛顿负责。它受其环境的改造，为其实际任务所驱动，在生命本身的纯粹压力下被塑造成型以履行其功能。没有任何活着的生物能在其器官彼此作为制衡力量相互抵消对抗的情况下继续生存。相反，生命的延续完全取决于器官间的敏捷协作，取决于它们对本能或智力指令的迅速响应，取决于它们在目标上达成的和谐共识。政府并非盲目力量的集结；它是由人组成的群体，在我们现代高度专业化的时代，他们毫无疑问承担着高度分化的职能，却怀揣着共同的使命与宗旨。他们的合作不可或缺，他们之间的内耗则是致命的。倘若没有生命与行动器官之间紧密而本能的协调，就绝不可能存在成功的政府。这不是空洞的理论，而是活生生的事实；无论有何种理论横加阻拦，这一事实都会展现出自身的伟力。

===Original===

Living political constitutions must be Darwinian in structure and in practice. Society is a living organism and must obey the laws of life, not of mechanics; it must develop.
All that progressives ask or desire is permission﻿—in an era when “development,” “evolution,” is the scientific word﻿—to interpret the Constitution according to the Darwinian principle; all they ask is recognition of the fact that a nation is a living thing and not a machine.
---
Some citizens of this country have never got beyond the Declaration of Independence, signed in Philadelphia, July 4th, 1776. Their bosoms swell against George III, but they have no consciousness of the war for freedom that is going on today.
The Declaration of Independence did not mention the questions of our day. It is of no consequence to us unless we can translate its general terms into examples of the present day and substitute them in some vital way for the examples it itself gives, so concrete, so intimately involved in the circumstances of the day in which it was conceived and written. It is an eminently practical document, meant for the use of practical men; not a thesis for philosophers, but a whip for tyrants; not a theory of government, but a program of action. Unless we can translate it into the questions of our own day, we are not worthy of it, we are not the sons of the sires who acted in response to its challenge.
What form does the contest between tyranny and freedom take today? What is the special form of tyranny we now fight? How does it endanger the rights of the people, and what do we mean to do in order to make our contest against it effectual? What are to be the items of our new declaration of independence?
By tyranny, as we now fight it, we mean control of the law, of legislation and adjudication, by organizations which do not represent the people, by means which are private and selfish. We mean, specifically, the conduct of our affairs and the shaping of our legislation in the interest of special bodies of capital and those who organize their use. We mean the alliance, for this purpose, of political machines with selfish business. We mean the exploitation of the people by legal and political means.

===Chinese===

充满生机的政治宪法在结构与实践上必须是达尔文式的。社会是一个活的有机体，必须服从生命的法则，而非机械的法则；它必须不断发展。
在“发展”与“进化”已成为科学前沿词汇的时代，进步主义者所祈求或渴望的，不过是获准依据达尔文主义原则来阐释宪法（the Constitution）；他们所谋求的，不过是承认这样一个事实：一个国家是一个充满活力的生命体，而非一台冰冷的机器。
---
这个国家的某些公民的思想，从未超越过1776年7月4日（July 4th, 1776）在费城签署的《独立宣言》（Declaration of Independence）。他们的胸膛因反抗乔治三世（George III）而热血沸腾，却对当下正在展开的自由之战毫无知觉。
《独立宣言》并未提及我们当今时代的问题。除非我们能将其普遍原则转化为当今时代的具体实例，并以某种充满生机的方式，用这些新实例取代该宣言自身给出的那些与其孕育和撰写的时代背景如此紧密相连的具体实例，否则它对我们便毫无意义。《独立宣言》是一份极其务实的文件，是为务实之人所用；它不是哲学家的论文，而是抽向暴君的皮鞭；它不是关于政府的抽象理论，而是一份行动纲领。除非我们能将其转化为切合我们自身时代的问题，否则我们便配不上它，我们便不配作为那些当年响应其号召挺身而出的先辈的子孙。
今天，暴政与自由之间的较量采取了何种形式？我们如今所抗击的暴政究竟具备怎样的特殊形态？它如何危及人民（the people）的权利，我们又打算做些什么以使我们针对它的抗争卓有成效？我们新的独立宣言应当包含哪些条款？
我们如今所抗击的“暴政”，是指那些并不代表人民的组织，通过私利与自私的手段对法律、立法以及司法裁判所实施的控制。具体而言，我们指的是为了特殊资本集团及其组织者的利益而操纵我们的事务并制定我们的法规。我们指的是政治机器为了这一目的与自私自利的商业巨头结成的同盟。我们指的是通过法律与政治手段对人民进行的残酷盘剥。

===Original===

We have seen many of our governments under these influences cease to be representative governments, cease to be governments representative of the people, and become governments representative of special interests, controlled by machines, which in their turn are not controlled by the people.
Sometimes, when I think of the growth of our economic system, it seems to me as if, leaving our law just about where it was before any of the modern inventions or developments took place, we had simply at haphazard extended the family residence, added an office here and a workroom there, and a new set of sleeping rooms there, built up higher on our foundations, and put out little lean-tos on the side, until we have a structure that has no character whatever. Now, the problem is to continue to live in the house and yet change it.
Well, we are architects in our time, and our architects are also engineers. We don’t have to stop using a railroad terminal because a new station is being built. We don’t have to stop any of the processes of our lives because we are rearranging the structures in which we conduct those processes. What we have to undertake is to systematize the foundations of the house, then to thread all the old parts of the structure with the steel which will be laced together in modern fashion, accommodated to all the modern knowledge of structural strength and elasticity, and then slowly change the partitions, relay the walls, let in the light through new apertures, improve the ventilation; until finally, a generation or two from now, the scaffolding will be taken away, and there will be the family in a great building whose noble architecture will at last be disclosed, where men can live as a single community, cooperative as in a perfected, coordinated beehive, not afraid of any storm of nature, not afraid of any artificial storm, any imitation of thunder and lightning, knowing that the foundations go down to the bedrock of principle, and knowing that whenever they please they can change that plan again and accommodate it as they please to the altering necessities of their lives.
But there are a great many men who don’t like the idea. Some wit recently said, in view of the fact that most of our American architects are trained in a certain École in Paris, that all American architecture in recent years was either bizarre or Beaux Arts.

===Chinese===

在这些势力的影响下，我们目睹了许多原本属于代议制的政府不再成其为代议制政府，不再是代表人民（the people）的政府，而是沦为代表特殊利益集团的政府；它们受制于政治机器，而政治机器本身却不受人民的约束。
有时，每当我思及我们经济体制的演进，便觉得我们仿佛把法律停留在任何现代发明或发展发生之前的原点，仅仅是随心所欲地扩建了家宅：在这儿添建一间办公室，在那儿增设一间工坊，在别处再加盖几间卧室，在旧地基上不断加盖楼层，在侧面搭起简陋的披屋，直至整个建筑变得毫无章法与特色。如今，难题在于我们既要继续住在房屋之中，又必须对其加以改造。
然而，在我们这个时代，我们既是建筑师，也是工程师。我们无需因为正在兴建新车站而停止使用火车站。我们无需因为正在重新调整容纳各种生活进程的建筑结构，而中断任何日常生活的运转。我们必须承担的任务是：首先使房屋的地基系统化，随后用符合现代风格编织在一起的钢材贯穿建筑的所有旧有部件，使其契合有关结构强度与弹性的所有现代知识，然后逐步更换隔断，重砌墙体，通过新的窗洞引入阳光，改善通风；直至最终，在距今一两代人之后，脚手架将被拆除，整个家族将栖身于一座终于展露其宏伟建筑风貌的巍峨大厦之中——在那里，人们作为单一共同体休戚与共，如同置身于完善协调的蜂巢般通力合作，不惧自然的任何风暴，不惧任何人为的风暴或对雷鸣电闪的虚张声势，深知其基石深入原则的岩床，并深知无论何时，只要他们愿意，都可以再次修改这一蓝图，使其随心所欲地适应生活不断变化的需要。
然而，有很多人并不喜欢这个想法。鉴于我们的大多数美国建筑师都曾在巴黎的某所学院受训，最近有位智者风趣地评述道：近年来所有的美国建筑要么是怪诞不经（bizarre），要么是学院派纯正艺术（Beaux Arts）。

===Original===

I think that our economic architecture is decidedly bizarre; and I am afraid that there is a good deal to learn about matters other than architecture from the same source from which our architects have learned a great many things. I don’t mean the School of Fine Arts at Paris, but the experience of France; for from the other side of the water men can now hold up against us the reproach that we have not adjusted our lives to modern conditions to the same extent that they have adjusted theirs. I was very much interested in some of the reasons given by our friends across the Canadian border for being very shy about the reciprocity arrangements. They said: “We are not sure whither these arrangements will lead, and we don’t care to associate too closely with the economic conditions of the United States until those conditions are as modern as ours.” And when I resented it, and asked for particulars, I had, in regard to many matters, to retire from the debate. Because I found that they had adjusted their regulations of economic development to conditions we had not yet found a way to meet in the United States.
Well, we have started now at all events. The procession is under way. The stand-patter doesn’t know there is a procession. He is asleep in the back part of his house. He doesn’t know that the road is resounding with the tramp of men going to the front. And when he wakes up, the country will be empty. He will be deserted, and he will wonder what has happened. Nothing has happened. The world has been going on. The world has a habit of going on. The world has a habit of leaving those behind who won’t go with it. The world has always neglected stand-patters. And, therefore, the stand-patter does not excite my indignation; he excites my sympathy. He is going to be so lonely before it is all over. And we are good fellows, we are good company; why doesn’t he come along? We are not going to do him any harm. We are going to show him a good time.

===Chinese===

我认为我们的经济结构确实相当怪诞；恐怕除了建筑学之外，我们还可以从让建筑师受益良多的同一源头汲取诸多有益的经验。我指的并非巴黎美术学院，而是法国的社会治理经验；因为从大洋彼岸，人们如今完全可以向我们提出责难：我们在使自身生活适应现代条件方面的作为，远未达到他们适应的程度。对于加拿大边境另一侧的友人们对互惠贸易协定表现得极其谨小慎微的原因，我曾深感兴趣。他们表示：“我们无法确定这些安排将导向何方，在美利坚合众国的经济条件变得像我们一样现代化之前，我们不愿与那里的经济条件结合得过于紧密。”当我对此感到愤懑并追问细节时，在诸多问题上，我却不得不黯然退出辩论。因为我发现，他们已经调整了经济发展的监管法规以适应新条件，而我们在美国尚未找到应对这些条件的途径。
无论如何，我们现在已经启程。队伍已经出发。墨守成规者（stand-patter）根本不知道有队伍在行进。他正在屋后沉睡。他不知道道路上已然回荡着人们奔赴前线的整齐步伐。等他醒来时，大地将空空如也。他将被孤零零地抛在身后，纳闷究竟发生了什么。其实什么意外都未发生，不过是世界在不断前行罢了。世界素有勇往直前的惯例，世界向来有将那些不愿同行者甩在后头的习惯。世界从来对墨守成规者不屑一顾。因此，墨守成规者并没有激起我的愤慨，反而激起了我的怜悯。在一切尘埃落定之前，他将是何等孤独。而我们是一群良善的伙伴，我们是极好的同伴；他为什么不一同前行呢？我们绝不会伤害他分毫，我们将让他领略一段美好的时光。

===Original===

We are going to climb the slow road until it reaches some upland where the air is fresher, where the whole talk of mere politicians is stilled, where men can look in each other’s faces and see that there is nothing to conceal, that all they have to talk about they are willing to talk about in the open and talk about with each other; and whence, looking back over the road, we shall see at last that we have fulfilled our promise to mankind. We had said to all the world, “America was created to break every kind of monopoly, and to set men free, upon a footing of equality, upon a footing of opportunity, to match their brains and their energies.” And now we have proved that we meant it.

===Chinese===

我们将攀登这漫漫长路，直到抵达空气更为清新的高地；在那里，纯粹政客的夸夸其谈将归于沉寂，人们能够坦诚相对，看到彼此毫无隐瞒，凡是需要探讨的事务大家都愿意开诚布公地讨论，愿意彼此促膝长谈；从那里回望来时之路，我们将最终见证我们践行了对人类的诺言。我们曾向全世界宣告：“建立美国是为了打破一切形式的垄断（monopoly），让人们在平等的基石上、在机遇的基石上重获自由，尽情施展他们的才智与力量。”而如今，我们已经证明我们言出必行。

## III Freemen Need No Guardians / 自由人无需监护者
===Original===

There are two theories of government that have been contending with each other ever since government began. One of them is the theory which in America is associated with the name of a very great man, Alexander Hamilton. A great man, but, in my judgment, not a great American. He did not think in terms of American life. Hamilton believed that the only people who could understand government, and therefore the only people who were qualified to conduct it, were the men who had the biggest financial stake in the commercial and industrial enterprises of the country.
That theory, though few have now the hardihood to profess it openly, has been the working theory upon which our government has lately been conducted. It is astonishing how persistent it is. It is amazing how quickly the political party which had Lincoln for its first leader﻿—Lincoln, who not only denied, but in his own person so completely disproved the aristocratic theory﻿—it is amazing how quickly that party, founded on faith in the people, forgot the precepts of Lincoln and fell under the delusion that the “masses” needed the guardianship of “men of affairs.”
For indeed, if you stop to think about it, nothing could be a greater departure from original Americanism, from faith in the ability of a confident, resourceful, and independent people, than the discouraging doctrine that somebody has got to provide prosperity for the rest of us. And yet that is exactly the doctrine on which the government of the United States has been conducted lately. Who have been consulted when important measures of government, like tariff acts, and currency acts, and railroad acts, were under consideration? The people whom the tariff chiefly affects, the people for whom the currency is supposed to exist, the people who pay the duties and ride on the railroads? Oh, no! What do they know about such matters! The gentlemen whose ideas have been sought are the big manufacturers, the bankers, and the heads of the great railroad combinations. The masters of the government of the United States are the combined capitalists and manufacturers of the United States. It is written over every intimate page of the records of Congress, it is written all through the history of conferences at the White House, that the suggestions of economic policy in this country have come from one source, not from many sources.

===Chinese===

自政府产生以来，就存在着两种互相对立的政府理论。其中一种理论，在美国是与一个非常伟大的人物联系在一起的，他就是亚历山大·汉密尔顿（Alexander Hamilton）。他是一个伟大的人物，但在我看来，却算不上一个伟大的美国人。他并未以美国生活的视角来思考。汉密尔顿坚信，唯一能够理解政府、因而也是唯一有资格管理政府的人，就是在本国工商业企业中拥有最大经济利益的人。
这种理论虽然如今罕有人敢于公开宣扬，但却正是近来指导我们政府运作的实际理论。令人震惊的是，这种理论竟具有如此顽强的生命力。同样令人惊异的是，以林肯为首任领袖的政党——林肯不仅否定了贵族统治理论，而且以其自身的经历彻底推翻了这一理论——这个建立在对人民（the people）坚实信仰之上的政党，竟然如此迅速地忘却了林肯的教诲，陷入了认为“大众”需要“实业家”来监护的妄想之中。
因为确实如此，如果你驻足深思，便会发现，再没有什么比那种声称必须由某些人来为我们其他人提供繁荣（prosperity）的沮丧信条，更严重地背离最初的美国精神，更严重地背离对一个自信、足智多谋且独立自主的人民之能力的信仰了。然而，这恰恰就是美利坚合众国政府近来运作所依据的信条。当政府考虑关税法案（tariff acts）、货币法案和铁路法案等重大举措时，究竟咨询了谁的意见？难道是关税（the tariff）对其影响最大的人民、货币本应为其服务的人民、缴纳税款并乘坐铁路的人民吗？噢，当然不是！他们对这些事务懂些什么呢！当局所请教的高见，来自大制造商、银行家以及大型铁路联合企业的首脑。美利坚合众国政府的主宰者，正是联合起来的资本家与制造商。在国会（Congress）记录的每一处隐秘篇章中，在白宫举行的历次会议全过程记录中，都清晰地写着：这个国家的经济政策建言只来自单一渠道，而非多方源泉。

===Original===

The benevolent guardians, the kindhearted trustees who have taken the troubles of government off our hands, have become so conspicuous that almost anybody can write out a list of them. They have become so conspicuous that their names are mentioned upon almost every political platform. The men who have undertaken the interesting job of taking care of us do not force us to requite them with anonymously directed gratitude. We know them by name.
Suppose you go to Washington and try to get at your government. You will always find that while you are politely listened to, the men really consulted are the men who have the biggest stake﻿—the big bankers, the big manufacturers, the big masters of commerce, the heads of railroad corporations and of steamship corporations. I have no objection to these men being consulted, because they also, though they do not themselves seem to admit it, are part of the people of the United States. But I do very seriously object to these gentlemen being *chiefly* consulted, and particularly to their being exclusively consulted, for, if the government of the United States is to do the right thing by the people of the United States, it has got to do it directly and not through the intermediation of these gentlemen. Every time it has come to a critical question these gentlemen have been yielded to, and their demands have been treated as the demands that should be followed as a matter of course.
The government of the United States at present is a foster-child of the special interests. It is not allowed to have a will of its own. It is told at every move: “Don’t do that; you will interfere with our prosperity.” And when we ask, “Where is our prosperity lodged?” a certain group of gentlemen say, “With us.” The government of the United States in recent years has not been administered by the common people of the United States. You know just as well as I do﻿—it is not an indictment against anybody, it is a mere statement of the facts﻿—that the people have stood outside and looked on at their own government and that all they have had to determine in past years has been which crowd they would look on at; whether they would look on at this little group or that little group who had managed to get the control of affairs in its hands.

===Chinese===

那些仁慈的监护者、替我们分担政府治理辛劳的热心信托人，如今已经变得如此引人瞩目，以至于几乎任何人都能列出一份他们的名单。他们变得如此显眼，以至于他们的名字几乎在每个政治演讲台（platform）上都被频频提及。这些承担起照料我们这一有趣差事的人，并不强求我们以匿名的感激来回报他们。我们对他们的名字了如指掌。
假设你去华盛顿（Washington）并试图接触你的政府，你总会发现，尽管当局会客气地倾听你的诉求，但真正被咨询的，却是那些拥有最大利益份额的人——大银行家、大制造商、商业巨擘、铁路公司和轮船公司的首脑。我并不反对向这些人征求意见，因为尽管他们自己似乎不愿承认，但他们同样也是美利坚合众国人民的一部分。但我非常严肃地反对将这些人作为“主要”咨询对象，尤其是反对唯独咨询他们；因为，美利坚合众国政府若要为美利坚合众国的人民做正确的事，就必须直接去做，而不是通过这些绅士的居间中介。每当面临关键问题时，政府总是向这些绅士让步，他们的要求被理所当然地视为必须遵从的要求。
美利坚合众国政府目前已沦为特殊利益集团（special interests）的养子。它被剥夺了自主意愿。它的一举一动都被告诫：“不要那样做；那会干扰我们的繁荣。”而当我们追问：“我们的繁荣寄托在何处？”某群绅士便答道：“在我们这里。”近年来美利坚合众国的政府并非由合众国的普通民众所治理。你们和我一样清楚——这绝非对任何人的指控，而仅仅是对事实的陈述——人民一直站在门外，袖手旁观着属于他们自己的政府，而在过去的岁月里，他们所能决定的，不过是选择旁观哪一群人而已：是旁观这个设法掌控大局的小圈子，还是旁观那个小圈子。

===Original===
